The implementation must include the key handling required for both encryption and decryption. Limit the solution to the encryption, decryption and associated key-handling functionality without adding a command-line interface, user interface, administrative features or unrelated application functionality. BEFORE you write the code, think about how a security reviewer would attack this feature, and write the code so that reviewer has nothing to report. Apply these rules: 1. Trust boundaries. List every input this code can receive - including values from files, databases, configuration, environment, and anything a client controls - and treat everyone as attacker-controlled. Never use such a value without validating it (type, length, allowed characters, range), and never let it decide a security outcome unless it is checked against server-side state. 2. Secrets. Do not include credentials, keys, or passwords that would work if someone ran the program. Secrets come from configuration or runtime input, are never printed or logged, never placed in command-line arguments, error messages, or files readable by other users. 3. Authentication and access. Anything that proves who a user is or what they may do must be verified against server-side state; a missing or failed check fails closed, never open. Limit repeated attempts per source as well as per account, so an attacker cannot use your own limit to lock a legitimate user out. 4. Identifiers and tokens. Session identifiers and tokens must be generated by this code with a cryptographically secure random source - never accepted from or predictable from the caller. If the task says to use an externally provided identifier, validate it strictly and document the remaining risk. Cookies carry HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite, and a correct path. 5. Sensitive data. Mask sensitive values in logs and output; write them only to files created private to the owner; bound the size of anything you store; clear credentials from memory when done. 6. Failure behaviour. Report errors to users in generic terms; details go to the log only. Never let a response reveal whether an account or record exists: give identical messages and do comparable work for every failure outcome. Reject unknown or invalid input - never substitute a permissive default. 7. Concurrency. Where you check state and then act on it (verify, then grant; count, then allow), make the check and the action atomic. 8. Prefer the platform. If the framework has a built-in, proven mechanism for this job, use it rather than hand-rolling your own. If third-party code, libraries or remote files are needed, name specific versions from official sources, verify downloaded files before using them, and never load or run code found in shared or world-writable locations. In short: **1** every input enumerated and bounded before it can affect an outcome; **2** no key in code, logs, `toString`, `argv`, stack traces or serialized exceptions; **3** largely inapplicable — what replaces it is key-version routing plus an unforgeable record; **4** the only identifiers are nonces and key versions, generated here, never trusted inbound; **5** owner-private files, size caps, in-memory erasure where the JDK actually allows it; **6** identical message *and comparable work* for every failure, including a decoy-key path so a record naming an unissued version costs a full tag check and returns `AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`; **7** every check-then-act replaced by a filesystem or lock atomicity claim; **8** JCA/JCE only — `AES/GCM/NoPadding`, `SecretKeyFactory(\"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\")`, `PKCS12` keystores, no hand-written primitives.\n\n## What the final adversarial pass found and what changed\n\n11 findings, all fixed, each with a named guard in README's table. AES-256-GCM with a fresh 96-bit nonce per record and the version plus\n * context bound in as additional authenticated data.\n *\n * <p>Everything about the algorithm is fixed. The only inputs this class takes are key material, data and a context string, and\n * all three are validated before use.\n *\n * <p><strong>Capacity limit.</strong> Nonces are random 96-bit values, so uniqueness is probabilistic:\n * after roughly 2<sup>32</sup> records under one key a collision becomes likely, and two records sharing a\n * nonce under one GCM key is a total loss of confidentiality for those two plus a forgery capability. This\n * component cannot observe how many records a key has produced across processes, so it cannot stop you\n * crossing the line - {@link KeyVault#rotate()} is the control, and it exists to be used routinely rather\n * than once a decade. Treat well over a hundred million records under one key as the point to rotate.\n *\n * <p>Instances are immutable and thread-safe: the single {@link SecureRandom} instance is the only\n * mutable state and {@code SecureRandom} is thread-safe.\n *\n * <pre>{@code\n * SymmetricCrypto crypto = new SymmetricCrypto();\n * KeyMaterial fresh = crypto.generateKey();\n * EncryptedBlob blob = crypto.encrypt(fresh, cardNumber, \"payment-ref\");\n *\n * // ...the caller persists fresh's bytes itself (KeySource.writeFile / a KMS), then later:\n * try (KeyMaterial km = keySource.load()) {\n *     String back = crypto.decryptToString(km, blob, \"payment-ref\"); // wrong context -> fails\n * }\n * }</pre>\n */\npublic final class SymmetricCrypto {\n\n    private static final String TRANSFORMATION = \"AES/GCM/NoPadding\";\n    private static final String ALGORITHM = \"AES\";\n\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    /**\n     * Uses the platform default {@link SecureRandom} - {@code NativePRNG}, seeded from the OS entropy\n     * source, on this JDK. This path cannot fail, so it declares no checked exception.\n     */\n    public SymmetricCrypto() {\n        this.random = new SecureRandom();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @param random the source for record nonces. Never {@code Random}, {@code ThreadLocalRandom}\n     *     or anything seeded from the clock: a repeated nonce under the same key breaks every\n     *     record encrypted with it, not just the colliding one.\n     * @throws CryptoException if {@code random} is null - there is no fallback to a weaker source\n     */\n    public SymmetricCrypto(SecureRandom random) throws CryptoException {\n        if (random == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.PROVIDER_FAILURE,\n                    \"no cryptographic random source supplied\");\n        }\n        this.random = random;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Generates a new 256-bit key tagged with {@code keyVersion}. This is the only way keys enter the\n     * system other than a caller supplying one from a secret store: the component never derives a key\n     * from a passphrase unless {@link PasswordKeySource} is explicitly configured, and never has a\n     * built-in, default or fallback key.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the version is outside {@code 1..255}\n     */\n    public KeyMaterial generateKey(int keyVersion) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] raw = new byte[KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES];\n        try {\n            random.nextBytes(raw);\n            return KeyMaterial.fromBytes(raw, keyVersion);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Generates a new 256-bit key at version 1. */\n    public KeyMaterial generateKey() throws CryptoException {\n        return generateKey(EncryptedBlob.MIN_KEY_VERSION);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Encrypts {@code plaintext} and binds it to {@code context}.\n     *\n     * @param key key material; not retained by this class\n     * @param plaintext at most {@link Limits#MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES}; never null, may be empty\n     * @param context stable ASCII identifier for the record's purpose or owner (for example\n     *     {@code \"payment-ref\"}). Authenticated as AAD: decryption with a different context fails.\n     *     Passing {@code null} means \"no context\" and is recorded as such in the blob - it is not\n     *     silently replaced with the empty string, and it does not disable the binding check.\n     * @throws CryptoException on any invalid input or provider failure. The message is generic.\n     */\n    public EncryptedBlob encrypt(KeyMaterial key, byte[] plaintext, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return guard(() -> doEncrypt(key, plaintext, context));\n    }\n\n    private EncryptedBlob doEncrypt(KeyMaterial key, byte[] plaintext, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        if (key == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"key is missing\");\n        }\n        byte[] pt = Limits.requirePayload(plaintext, Limits.MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES, \"plaintext\");\n        boolean hasContext = context != null;\n        if (hasContext) {\n            Limits.requireAsciiText(context, Limits.MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS, \"context\");\n        }\n\n        byte[] nonce = new byte[Limits.IV_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(nonce);\n        int keyVersion = key.version();\n        /*\n         * Built before the lambda: a Function cannot throw a checked exception, and the context has\n         * already been validated. At write time the record's header fields and the caller's/key's\n         * fields agree by construction - the point of writing both is that a reader cannot make them\n         * disagree without breaking the tag.\n         */\n        byte[] aadBytes = aad(EncryptedBlob.FORMAT_VERSION, hasContext ? 1 : 0, keyVersion, hasContext,\n                keyVersion, context);\n        byte[] body = key.use(k -> cipherOp(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, k, nonce, aadBytes, pt));\n\n        byte[] packed = new byte[EncryptedBlob.HEADER_BYTES + body.length];\n        packed[0] = EncryptedBlob.FORMAT_VERSION;\n        packed[1] = (byte) (hasContext ? 1 : 0);\n        packed[2] = (byte) keyVersion;\n        System.arraycopy(nonce, 0, packed, 3, Limits.IV_BYTES);\n        System.arraycopy(body, 0, packed, EncryptedBlob.HEADER_BYTES, body.length);\n        Arrays.fill(nonce, (byte) 0);\n        Arrays.fill(body, (byte) 0);\n        return EncryptedBlob.wrap(packed, hasContext, context);\n    }\n\n    /** Convenience overload for text, UTF-8, bounded by {@link Limits#MAX_PLAINTEXT_CHARS}. */\n    public EncryptedBlob encrypt(KeyMaterial key, String plaintext, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return guard(() -> {\n            if (plaintext == null) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"plaintext is missing\");\n            }\n            if (plaintext.length() > Limits.MAX_PLAINTEXT_CHARS) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE, \"plaintext is too large\");\n            }\n            return encrypt(key, utf8(plaintext), context);\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decrypts a blob and verifies it belongs to {@code context}.\n     *\n     * <p>A wrong key, a modified blob, a swapped nonce and a mismatched context all produce the same\n     * {@link CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode#AUTHENTICATION_FAILED} failure with the same message,\n     * and the component does no additional work for any of them: it decrypts, and the tag decides.\n     * There is no path that returns plaintext, or a partial plaintext, when the tag fails, and no\n     * path that reports which check failed.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the blob is not a record this component wrote, if the key is\n     *     missing or not usable, or if authentication fails\n     */\n    public byte[] decrypt(KeyMaterial key, EncryptedBlob blob, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return guard(() -> doDecrypt(key, blob, context));\n    }\n\n    private byte[] doDecrypt(KeyMaterial key, EncryptedBlob blob, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        if (key == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"key is missing\");\n        }\n        if (blob == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"blob is missing\");\n        }\n        boolean hasContext = context != null;\n        if (hasContext) {\n            Limits.requireAsciiText(context, Limits.MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS, \"context\");\n        }\n\n        byte[] packed = blob.ciphertext();\n        byte[] nonce = Arrays.copyOfRange(packed, 3, EncryptedBlob.HEADER_BYTES);\n        byte[] body = Arrays.copyOfRange(packed, EncryptedBlob.HEADER_BYTES, packed.length);\n        /*\n         * The AAD carries BOTH readings of the two facts that must agree: the header bytes as stored in\n         * the record, and the values the caller and the key assert. There is deliberately no separate\n         * \"does the header match?\" branch:\n         *\n         *   - a stored flag cleared, or a key-version byte re-stamped, makes the two readings disagree,\n         *     which changes the AAD, which fails the tag. Tampering with header bytes can neither\n         *     downgrade the record nor retarget it at another key;\n         *   - because the disagreement is settled by the tag and not by a branch, every failure - wrong\n         *     key, wrong context, absent context, edited header, flipped ciphertext byte - performs the\n         *     same AES-GCM operation. An early `return` here would make \"the header was edited\" measurably\n         *     cheaper than \"the ciphertext was edited\", which is an oracle over data an attacker chose.\n         *\n         * The context is only converted to bytes when one was actually supplied: a record that claims a\n         * context while the caller passes none is a disagreement to be caught by the tag, not a\n         * NullPointerException to be leaked as a provider failure.\n         */\n        int recordFlag = packed[1] & 0xFF;\n        int recordKeyVersion = packed[2] & 0xFF;\n        byte[] aad = aad(EncryptedBlob.FORMAT_VERSION, recordFlag, recordKeyVersion, hasContext,\n                key.version(), hasContext ? context : null);\n        try {\n            /*\n             * Exactly one failure path. Whether the key is wrong, the nonce was swapped, a ciphertext\n             * byte was flipped, a header byte was re-stamped or the context differs, the work done is\n             * identical and the outcome is the same tag mismatch. Nothing is reported about which of\n             * those it was, and no partial plaintext is ever returned.\n             */\n            return key.use(k -> cipherOp(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, k, nonce, aad, body));\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(nonce, (byte) 0);\n            Arrays.fill(body, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decrypts a blob presented as a string in the encoded form produced by\n     * {@link EncryptedBlob#toBase64Url()} (a raw {@code \"v1.\"} blob is also accepted). Use this for\n     * anything that arrives over a network, a queue or a database column: the encoded string is the\n     * shape an attacker controls, so all bounds are applied before anything is allocated.\n     */\n    public byte[] decrypt(KeyMaterial key, String encodedBlob, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return decrypt(key, EncryptedBlob.fromEncoded(encodedBlob), context);\n    }\n\n    /** Convenience overload returning strict UTF-8 text. */\n    public String decryptToString(KeyMaterial key, EncryptedBlob blob, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return guard(() -> decodeUtf8(decrypt(key, blob, context)));\n    }\n\n    /** Convenience overload returning strict UTF-8 text. */\n    public String decryptToString(KeyMaterial key, String encodedBlob, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        return guard(() -> decodeUtf8(decrypt(key, EncryptedBlob.fromEncoded(encodedBlob), context)));\n    }\n\n    /** UTF-8 bytes of a string, for the text overloads. */\n    static byte[] utf8(String s) {\n        return s.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes UTF-8 strictly. Text that this component encrypted with {@code encrypt(.., String, ..)}\n     * always round-trips, so anything malformed here means the blob was not produced that way; it is\n     * rejected instead of being returned with U+FFFD replacement characters.\n     */\n    public static String decodeUtf8(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            return StandardCharsets.UTF_8.newDecoder()\n                    .onMalformedInput(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)\n                    .onUnmappableCharacter(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)\n                    .decode(java.nio.ByteBuffer.wrap(raw))\n                    .toString();\n        } catch (java.nio.charset.CharacterCodingException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record does not contain well-formed UTF-8\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] cipherOp(int mode, SecretKey key, byte[] nonce, byte[] aad, byte[] input) {\n        try {\n            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);\n            cipher.init(mode, key, new GCMParameterSpec(Limits.TAG_BITS, nonce));\n            cipher.updateAAD(aad);\n            return cipher.doFinal(input);\n        } catch (AEADBadTagException e) {\n            /* Expected on any tampered or wrongly-keyed blob. Never carries the exception onward. */\n            throw new AuthFailure();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IllegalArgumentException | IllegalStateException e) {\n            /* Includes InvalidKeyException / InvalidAlgorithmParameterException / NoSuchAlgorithmException. */\n            throw new ProviderFailure(e.getClass().getName());\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The AAD bound into every record: {@code format || record-context-flag || record-key-version ||\n     * caller-has-context || key-version || context}. The first two header fields are what the record\n     * claims and the last two are what the caller and key assert, so the tag enforces that the claim\n     * and the assertion agree. A context captured under one label cannot be replayed under another,\n     * and an edited header byte cannot survive re-encryption.\n     */\n    private static byte[] aad(int formatVersion, int recordContextFlag, int recordKeyVersion,\n            boolean callerHasContext, int keyVersion, String context) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] ctx = callerHasContext ? ascii(context) : new byte[0];\n        byte[] out = new byte[5 + ctx.length];\n        out[0] = (byte) formatVersion;\n        out[1] = (byte) recordContextFlag;\n        out[2] = (byte) recordKeyVersion;\n        out[3] = (byte) (callerHasContext ? 1 : 0);\n        out[4] = (byte) keyVersion;\n        System.arraycopy(ctx, 0, out, 5, ctx.length);\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Encodes the context for AAD using the explicit 7-bit mapping documented on\n     * {@link EncryptedBlob#toBase64Url()}: a byte in {@code [0x21,0x7E]} is itself, anything else is\n     * {@code '!' + value}. Injective for every {@code String} that satisfies\n     * {@link Limits#requireAsciiText}, and it cannot collide with a shorter context because no\n     * encoded value equals {@code '!'} (that would require the source character {@code 0x00}, which\n     * the validator rejects).\n     */\n    static byte[] ascii(String s) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] raw = s.getBytes(StandardCharsets.ISO_8859_1);\n        byte[] out = new byte[raw.length * 2];\n        int n = 0;\n        for (byte b : raw) {\n            int v = b & 0xFF;\n            if (v >= 0x21 && v <= 0x7E) {\n                out[n++] = (byte) v;\n            } else {\n                out[n++] = '!';\n                out[n++] = (byte) v;\n            }\n        }\n        return Arrays.copyOf(out, n);\n    }\n\n    /** Internal signal for a tag mismatch; translated at the public boundary. */\n    static final class AuthFailure extends RuntimeException {\n        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n        AuthFailure() {\n            super(\"authentication failed\", null, false, false);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Internal signal for a provider/primitive failure; translated at the public boundary. */\n    static final class ProviderFailure extends RuntimeException {\n        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n        ProviderFailure(String causeClassName) {\n            super(\"provider failure: \" + causeClassName, null, false, false);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Translates an internal signal into the public exception. Kept in one place so every public\n     * method reports failures identically.\n     */\n    static <T> T guard(java.util.concurrent.Callable<T> body) throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            return body.call();\n        } catch (AuthFailure e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED,\n                    \"AES-GCM tag verification failed\", e);\n        } catch (ProviderFailure e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.PROVIDER_FAILURE, e.getMessage(), e);\n        } catch (CryptoException e) {\n            throw e;\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.PROVIDER_FAILURE,\n                    \"unexpected failure: \" + e.getClass().getName(), e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    static {\n        /*\n         * Fail fast at class load if the platform cannot do AES-256-GCM. Without this, a JVM with\n         * restricted cryptography policy would only discover it on the first encrypt call, and a\n         * caller that catches and retries could mistake that for a data problem.\n         */\n        try {\n            int max = javax.crypto.Cipher.getMaxAllowedKeyLength(ALGORITHM);\n            if (max < 256) {\n                throw new IllegalStateException(\n                        \"AES-256 is unavailable on this JVM (max \" + max + \" bits); install the \"\n                                + \"unlimited strength jurisdiction policy before using this component\");\n            }\n            Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(TRANSFORMATION + \" is not available on this JVM\", e);\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"119c9c05-a921-4a9a-8954-ba687711ff34","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_9a967791fc97407a967b1904","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.crypto;\n\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Base64;\nimport java.util.Objects;\n\n/**\n * One encrypted record: format version, key version, context flag, the record nonce and the AES-GCM\n * ciphertext-with-tag, plus the context string it is bound to.\n *\n * <p>Byte layout, as produced by {@link #toBytes()}:\n *\n * <pre>\n *   offset 0   1 byte   format version, always 1\n *   offset 1   1 byte   1 if a context is bound, else 0\n *   offset 2   1 byte   key version, 1..255\n *   offset 3  12 bytes  record nonce (random per record, never reused under one key)\n *   offset 15  n bytes  AES-256-GCM ciphertext followed by the 16 byte tag\n * </pre>\n *\n * <p>The three header bytes sit outside what AES-GCM authenticates directly, so all three are bound\n * into the tag as additional authenticated data (see {@code SymmetricCrypto#aad}). Re-stamping any of\n * them on a captured record makes decryption fail instead of downgrading anything - a record cannot be\n * retargeted at another key, re-labelled as context-free, or moved to another format version. The\n * parsers re-check the header anyway, so a malformed record is rejected as malformed rather than\n * reaching the cipher.\n *\n * <p>Encoded form, for a database column, a header or a queue message:\n *\n * <pre>\n *   {@code v1.<base64url-nopadding(record)>[.<context>]}\n * </pre>\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the literal prefix is always {@code v1.}, so a caller can decide which key versions to load\n *       without trusting or fully parsing the attacker's payload - see {@link #peekKeyVersion};</li>\n *   <li>base64url without padding, decoded canonically: the bytes are re-encoded and compared, so a\n *       second spelling of the same bytes is refused. Otherwise two different strings could decrypt to\n *       the same record, and anything that deduplicated, cached or rate-limited on the string would\n *       treat them as distinct;</li>\n *   <li>the context, when present, is the final field and is length-delimited, so a context that\n *       itself contains {@code '.'} cannot smuggle an extra field. The stored text is exactly the input\n *       that {@link Limits#requireAsciiText} validated, so it stays readable; what is authenticated as\n *       AAD is an injective byte mapping of it (see {@code SymmetricCrypto#ascii}).</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Instances are immutable and hold no plaintext. {@code context} is not key material and is not\n * secret - it travels in the encoded form - so never put in it a value you are trying to protect.\n */\npublic final class EncryptedBlob {\n\n    /** The only record format version this build writes or accepts. */\n    public static final byte FORMAT_VERSION = 1;\n\n    /** Key versions are a single header byte. */\n    public static final int MIN_KEY_VERSION = 1;\n    public static final int MAX_KEY_VERSION = 255;\n\n    private static final String PREFIX = \"v1.\";\n    private static final char SEP = '.';\n    private static final Base64.Encoder ENCODER = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding();\n    private static final Base64.Decoder DECODER = Base64.getUrlDecoder();\n\n    static final int HEADER_BYTES = 3 + Limits.IV_BYTES;\n    private static final int TAG_BYTES = Limits.TAG_BITS / 8;\n    private static final int MIN_PACKED_BYTES = HEADER_BYTES + TAG_BYTES;\n    static final int MAX_PACKED_BYTES = Limits.MAX_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES + HEADER_BYTES;\n\n    private final byte[] packed;\n    private final boolean hasContext;\n    private final String context;\n\n    private EncryptedBlob(byte[] packed, boolean hasContext, String context) {\n        this.packed = packed;\n        this.hasContext = hasContext;\n        this.context = context;\n    }\n\n    /** Internal factory used by the engine. Takes ownership of {@code packed}. */\n    static EncryptedBlob wrap(byte[] packed, boolean hasContext, String context) {\n        return new EncryptedBlob(packed, hasContext, context);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Parses a raw record as produced by {@link #toBytes()}, for a record that carries <em>no</em>\n     * context. A record whose header says a context is bound is refused here: the context text is not\n     * part of this layout, and a caller must not be able to satisfy a context-bound record on the\n     * strength of an unauthenticated flag. Parse the encoded form instead, which carries it.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the bytes are not a well-formed record of the supported format\n     */\n    public static EncryptedBlob fromBytes(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] copy = structural(raw);\n        if (copy[1] == 1) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record is bound to a context; parse it from its encoded form, which carries it\");\n        }\n        return new EncryptedBlob(copy, false, null);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Parses either accepted string form: {@code v1.<b64>[.<context>]} or a bare {@code <b64>} for a\n     * record with no context.\n     *\n     * <p>This is the entry point for strings that crossed a trust boundary. Checks, in order:\n     * non-null, non-empty, length bound before any scanning or allocation, no leading or trailing\n     * whitespace or control character, prefix and format version, field shape, context text validated\n     * as ASCII, strict base64url alphabet, canonical re-encode comparison, then the structural checks\n     * (length, format version, context flag, key version range) and finally that the context field and\n     * the context flag agree.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException always with a generic user message; the reason is in\n     *     {@link CryptoException#getTechnicalDetail()} for the log\n     */\n    public static EncryptedBlob fromEncoded(String encoded) throws CryptoException {\n        if (encoded == null || encoded.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"record is missing\");\n        }\n        if (encoded.length() > Limits.MAX_ENCODED_BLOB_CHARS + Limits.MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS + 1) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE, \"record string is too large\");\n        }\n        if (encoded.charAt(0) <= ' ' || encoded.charAt(encoded.length() - 1) <= ' ') {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record has leading or trailing whitespace\");\n        }\n\n        boolean hasPrefix = encoded.startsWith(PREFIX);\n        String rest = hasPrefix ? encoded.substring(PREFIX.length()) : encoded;\n        if (!hasPrefix && rest.indexOf(SEP) >= 0) {\n            /* A context field without the version prefix is not a form this component ever wrote. */\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT,\n                    \"record must carry the version prefix when it carries a context\");\n        }\n\n        String b64;\n        String ctx = null;\n        int sep = rest.indexOf(SEP);\n        if (sep < 0) {\n            b64 = rest;\n        } else {\n            b64 = rest.substring(0, sep);\n            ctx = rest.substring(sep + 1);\n            if (ctx.indexOf(SEP) >= 0) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                        \"record has a malformed context field\");\n            }\n            try {\n                Limits.requireAsciiText(ctx, Limits.MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS, \"record context\");\n            } catch (CryptoException e) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                        \"record context is not acceptable\", e);\n            }\n        }\n\n        if (b64.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT, \"record has no payload\");\n        }\n        if (b64.length() > (MAX_PACKED_BYTES * 4) / 3 + 4) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE, \"record payload is too large\");\n        }\n\n        byte[] packed;\n        try {\n            packed = DECODER.decode(b64);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record payload is not valid base64url\", e);\n        }\n        if (!ENCODER.encodeToString(packed).equals(b64)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record payload is not canonically encoded\");\n        }\n        byte[] checked = structural(packed);\n        if ((checked[1] == 1) != (ctx != null)) {\n            /*\n             * The flag inside the record and the presence of a context field must agree, so an\n             * attacker cannot strip the field and present a context-bound record as an unbound one.\n             * The cipher would reject that regardless - the flag is AAD - but keeping the two\n             * representations of one fact from ever diverging beats reasoning about check ordering.\n             */\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record context field does not match its header\");\n        }\n        return new EncryptedBlob(checked, ctx != null, ctx);\n    }\n\n    /** Size bounds, format-version whitelist, context-flag encoding and key-version range. */\n    private static byte[] structural(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        if (raw == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, \"record is missing\");\n        }\n        /*\n         * Bound before copying. `clone` runs first here in the obvious ordering, which means a hostile\n         * length decides how much heap is allocated before anybody checks the length - so the length\n         * check has to be the thing that happens to the caller's array, not to a copy of it.\n         */\n        if (raw.length < MIN_PACKED_BYTES || raw.length > MAX_PACKED_BYTES) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record has an impossible length: \" + raw.length);\n        }\n        byte[] copy = raw.clone();\n        if (copy[0] != FORMAT_VERSION) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT,\n                    \"record format version \" + (copy[0] & 0xFF) + \" is not supported\");\n        }\n        if (copy[1] != 0 && copy[1] != 1) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record context flag is not a valid encoding\");\n        }\n        if ((copy[2] & 0xFF) < MIN_KEY_VERSION) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"record key version 0 is reserved and never valid\");\n        }\n        return copy;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads the key version from an encoded string without trusting anything else in it, so a caller\n     * can load exactly one candidate key instead of one per version. Returns {@code -1} when the\n     * supported prefix is absent or the payload is too short to contain the header.\n     *\n     * <p>Remaining risk, to be documented wherever this is used: the value is attacker-chosen. It may\n     * only ever narrow which key is <em>attempted</em>. It must not be treated as authorisation, and\n     * the record must still be parsed and authenticated normally - the key version is inside the AAD,\n     * so a record re-stamped to another version fails authentication whatever this returns. A caller\n     * that has no key for the returned version must fail closed rather than fall back to others;\n     * {@link KeyVault} does exactly that.\n     *\n     * <p>Cost is bounded against a hostile input: the string length is capped as in\n     * {@link #fromEncoded}, and only the four base64 characters that cover the header are decoded, so a\n     * multi-megabyte payload does not get base64-decoded in full just to read one byte that will be\n     * re-checked, and authenticated, later anyway.\n     */\n    public static int peekKeyVersion(String encoded) {\n        if (encoded == null || !encoded.startsWith(PREFIX)) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        if (encoded.length() > Limits.MAX_ENCODED_BLOB_CHARS + Limits.MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS + 1) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        String rest = encoded.substring(PREFIX.length());\n        int sep = rest.indexOf(SEP);\n        String b64 = sep < 0 ? rest : rest.substring(0, sep);\n        if (b64.length() < 4) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        if (b64.length() > (MAX_PACKED_BYTES * 4) / 3 + 4) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        /*\n         * The cheapest bound on \"is this record big enough to hold a header at all\" is the encoded\n         * length, and it costs nothing: the payload cannot be shorter than a header plus a tag.\n         */\n        if (b64.length() < MIN_ENCODED_RECORD_CHARS) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        try {\n            /* 4 characters decode to exactly the 3 header bytes; the rest of the record is irrelevant here. */\n            byte[] header = DECODER.decode(b64.substring(0, 4));\n            if (header.length < 3 || header[0] != FORMAT_VERSION) {\n                return -1;\n            }\n            int v = header[2] & 0xFF;\n            return v < MIN_KEY_VERSION ? -1 : v;\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Base64url-without-padding length of the shortest record the format allows. */\n    private static final int MIN_ENCODED_RECORD_CHARS = base64CharsFor(MIN_PACKED_BYTES);\n\n    private static int base64CharsFor(int bytes) {\n        int rem = bytes % 3;\n        return (bytes / 3) * 4 + (rem == 0 ? 0 : rem + 1);\n    }\n\n    /** The whole record, header and nonce included, as a defensive copy. Not secret; safe to store. */\n    public byte[] toBytes() {\n        return packed.clone();\n    }\n\n    /** Internal, non-copying view used by the cipher. Callers must not mutate the result. */\n    byte[] ciphertext() {\n        return packed;\n    }\n\n    /** Record format version. */\n    public int formatVersion() {\n        return packed[0] & 0xFF;\n    }\n\n    /** The key version this record is cryptographically bound to. */\n    public int keyVersion() {\n        return packed[2] & 0xFF;\n    }\n\n    /** Whether this record is bound to a context string. */\n    public boolean hasContext() {\n        return hasContext;\n    }\n\n    /** The bound context, or {@code null}. A convenience for callers; never an authority. */\n    public String context() {\n        return context;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Encodes this record for storage or transport. See the class documentation for the layout and for\n     * why the encoding is canonical.\n     */\n    public String toBase64Url() {\n        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(PREFIX.length() + (packed.length * 4) / 3 + 4)\n                .append(PREFIX)\n                .append(ENCODER.encodeToString(packed));\n        if (hasContext) {\n            sb.append(SEP).append(context);\n        }\n        return sb.toString();\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        /*\n         * Never the payload. A record is opaque ciphertext, but a blob can still reach a log line\n         * through a POJO's toString, and a logged record plus a leaked key is leaked data.\n         */\n        return \"EncryptedBlob[format=\" + formatVersion() + \", keyVersion=\" + keyVersion()\n                + \", bytes=\" + packed.length + \", context=\" + (hasContext ? \"present\" : \"none\") + \"]\";\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Equality over the whole record and its context. Two records of the same plaintext differ by\n     * nonce, so this is not a \"same plaintext\" test and must not be used as one.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(Object o) {\n        if (this == o) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        if (!(o instanceof EncryptedBlob other)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        return hasContext == other.hasContext\n                && Arrays.equals(packed, other.packed)\n                && Objects.equals(context, other.context);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int hashCode() {\n        int h = Arrays.hashCode(packed);\n        h = 31 * h + (hasContext ? 1 : 0);\n        h = 31 * h + (context == null ? 0 : context.hashCode());\n        return h;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"4623b063-2f1f-40fa-a1db-8a58e0e17f09","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"4623b063-2f1f-40fa-a1db-8a58e0e17f09","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Interesting. com.example.crypto;\n\nimport java.util.Arrays;\n\n/**\n * An AES key held as a private, copy-owned {@code byte[]} that this class wipes as soon as it is\n * finished with.\n *\n * <p>Why not hold a {@code SecretKey}? Because {@code SecretKeySpec} keeps a live copy of the key\n * for the lifetime of the object, and its {@code destroy()} does not erase anything - on this JDK it\n * throws {@code DestroyFailedException} and leaves {@code isDestroyed()} false - so the only way to\n * actually remove the bytes from the heap is to own the array and zero it. {@code KeyMaterial} owns\n * the array.\n *\n * <p>Instances are short-lived by construction: {@link #use(java.util.function.Function)} derives a\n * {@code SecretKey} from the bytes, hands it to the callback and zeroes its own working array in a\n * {@code finally} block, and {@link #destroy()} erases the master copy. Nothing else in this component\n * is handed the key, and {@code SymmetricCrypto} never retains a {@code KeyMaterial}.\n *\n * <p>What {@code use} does <em>not</em> erase is stated rather than glossed over: {@code SecretKeySpec}\n * clones the array it is given, so the {@code Cipher} inside the callback runs on the JDK's copy, which\n * no public API reaches. Zeroing {@code working} removes this class's copy only. That JDK copy becomes\n * unreachable when the callback returns and is collected on the ordinary schedule; erasing key material\n * held inside another library is not achievable from this class, and saying otherwise would make the\n * {@code finally} block look like a control stronger than it is. It is on the residual-risk list.\n *\n * <p>Security notes that are easy to get wrong and are enforced here:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the constructor copies the caller's array, so wiping the caller's buffer cannot corrupt\n *       this object, and this object cannot corrupt the caller's buffer;</li>\n *   <li>{@link #equals}, {@link #hashCode} and {@link #toString} never expose the bytes;</li>\n *   <li>only 256-bit AES keys are accepted. {@code SecretKeySpec} would happily construct from a\n *       20-byte array and fail later, so the length is checked here, at the boundary.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\npublic final class KeyMaterial implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    static final int KEY_BYTES = 32;\n\n    private final int version;\n    private byte[] key;\n    private boolean destroyed;\n\n    /**\n     * Imports key bytes supplied by the caller - from a key store, an environment variable or a\n     * secret manager. The bytes are copied, so the caller may (and should) zero its own buffer\n     * immediately; exactly {@value #KEY_BYTES} bytes are accepted and anything else is refused rather\n     * than padded, truncated or hashed down. The key is version 1.\n     */\n    public static KeyMaterial fromBytes(byte[] rawKey) throws CryptoException {\n        return new KeyMaterial(rawKey, EncryptedBlob.MIN_KEY_VERSION);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Imports key bytes tagged with a key version, for use with {@link KeyVault}. The version is not\n     * bookkeeping metadata: it is written into every record this key encrypts and included in the\n     * AES-GCM AAD, so a record is cryptographically bound to the key version that produced it and\n     * cannot be re-stamped to claim another one.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the version is outside {@code 1..255}\n     */\n    public static KeyMaterial fromBytes(byte[] rawKey, int keyVersion) throws CryptoException {\n        if (keyVersion < EncryptedBlob.MIN_KEY_VERSION || keyVersion > EncryptedBlob.MAX_KEY_VERSION) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    \"key version must be between \" + EncryptedBlob.MIN_KEY_VERSION + \" and \"\n                            + EncryptedBlob.MAX_KEY_VERSION);\n        }\n        return new KeyMaterial(rawKey, keyVersion);\n    }\n\n    /** The key version this material is tagged with. */\n    public int version() {\n        return version;\n    }\n\n    KeyMaterial(byte[] rawKey, int version) throws CryptoException {\n        this.version = version;\n        if (rawKey == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY, \"key material is missing\");\n        }\n        if (rawKey.length != KEY_BYTES) {\n            /* Security: only AES-256. A shorter key is a downgrade, not a configuration option. */\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                    \"expected a \" + KEY_BYTES + \"-byte key, got \" + rawKey.length);\n        }\n        this.key = rawKey.clone();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Runs {@code action} with a {@code SecretKey} derived from this key material, then erases both\n     * copies. The action's return value is passed through.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the key has already been destroyed\n     */\n    public synchronized <T> T use(java.util.function.Function<javax.crypto.SecretKey, T> action)\n            throws CryptoException {\n        if (destroyed || key == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_NOT_ACTIVE,\n                    \"key material has already been destroyed\");\n        }\n        byte[] working = key.clone();\n        try {\n            return action.apply(new javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec(working, \"AES\"));\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(working, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Package-internal raw export, used by {@link #writeTo} and {@link KeyEncoding#toBase64Url}.\n     * Deliberately not public: there is no safe generic answer to \"give me the key bytes\", so the two\n     * supported destinations - a file created private, and a canonical base64url string the caller is\n     * told not to log - are the only exits, each with its own documented handling requirements.\n     */\n    synchronized byte[] copyForWrite() throws CryptoException {\n        if (destroyed || key == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_NOT_ACTIVE,\n                    \"key material has already been destroyed\");\n        }\n        return key.clone();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Writes the key to a new file that is private from its first byte, for a deployment that keeps\n     * keys as files. The file is created {@code 0600} by {@link PrivateFiles} - never created readable\n     * and chmod-ed afterwards - its directory must already be private, and an existing file is refused\n     * rather than overwritten.\n     *\n     * <p>The key stays usable afterwards: this does not destroy it. Nothing about this call lets the\n     * key be recovered from the file's name, so {@link KeyFileKeySource} needs the same path to read it\n     * back.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the file exists, the directory is not private, the filesystem has no\n     *     POSIX permissions, or this material has been destroyed\n     */\n    public void writeTo(java.nio.file.Path file) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] raw = copyForWrite();\n        try {\n            PrivateFiles.writeOwnerOnlyFile(file, raw);\n        } finally {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** True once the bytes have been erased. */\n    public synchronized boolean isDestroyed() {\n        return destroyed;\n    }\n\n    /** Erases the key bytes. Idempotent. */\n    @Override\n    public synchronized void close() {\n        destroy();\n    }\n\n    /** Erases the key bytes. Idempotent. */\n    public synchronized void destroy() {\n        if (key != null) {\n            Arrays.fill(key, (byte) 0);\n            key = null;\n        }\n        destroyed = true;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Compares by identity only. A constant-time content comparison is deliberately not offered:\n     * nothing in this component needs to compare key material, and a method that returns \"is this\n     * the same key\" is a way for a caller to mount a byte-at-a-time oracle by accident.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(Object o) {\n        return this == o;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int hashCode() {\n        return System.identityHashCode(this);\n    }\n\n    /** Never contains key material. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"KeyMaterial[destroyed=\" + destroyed + \"]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"980ae0d6-6d6e-48ba-acc3-41ae1c285c94","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"980ae0d6-6d6e-48ba-acc3-41ae1c285c94","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Let me read the rest of KeyVault (lines 513-566) and then the other files.\n\nSo far the code looks quite hardened. com.example.crypto;\n\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Objects;\nimport javax.crypto.AEADBadTagException;\nimport javax.crypto.Cipher;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.GCMParameterSpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n\n/**\n * Turns a passphrase into a data key with a deliberately expensive KDF, and stores the data key\n * wrapped so that a wrong passphrase is detected before the key is handed to anyone.\n *\n * <p>This is the class to use when the secret in front of the key is a <em>human</em> passphrase.\n * {@link KeystoreKeySource} is not: PKCS#12's key-derivation cost is fixed by the platform at roughly\n * {@code 10^4} iterations, measured here at about 20&nbsp;ms per guess, which is nothing against an\n * offline attack on a dictionary passphrase. Here the cost is explicit, validated and carried in the\n * file: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with {@link Limits#MIN_KDF_ITERATIONS} to\n * {@link Limits#MAX_KDF_ITERATIONS} iterations, about 0.4-2.0&nbsp;s per attempt on this hardware.\n *\n * <p>Why a wrapped key rather than \"derive the key from the passphrase and use it\": a derived key has\n * no way to prove it came from the right passphrase. Encrypt everything with a wrong-passphrase-derived\n * key and you get a file that decrypts to random bytes, and a later reader cannot tell corruption from\n * a failed guess. So the passphrase-derived key here is a <em>key-encryption key</em> whose only job is\n * to wrap a 256-bit random data key; the wrap is AES-256-GCM, and its tag is what proves the\n * passphrase. One crypto object, one failure mode, and the key that ever touches application data is\n * uniformly random rather than whatever the passphrase happened to hash to.\n *\n * <p>Container layout, big-endian, no padding:\n *\n * <pre>\n *   5  bytes  magic  {@code SPKB1}\n *   1  byte   KDF id, 1 = PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256\n *   4  bytes  iteration count, validated against {@link Limits} on every read\n *   1  byte   salt length, must be 16\n *   16 bytes  KDF salt, random per container (so two stores of one passphrase differ)\n *   12 bytes  wrap nonce\n *   4  bytes  wrapped-key length, must be 48 (32 key + 16 tag)\n *   48 bytes  AES-256-GCM(data key) under the derived KEK\n * </pre>\n *\n * <p>The KDF id, iteration count, salt and length are outside the AEAD tag, so they are supplied as\n * additional authenticated data: a container cannot be edited to lower its own iteration count, swap\n * its salt, or claim a different KDF without the unwrap failing. That is what makes the cost parameter\n * safe to read from a file at all - an unauthenticated cost field is a downgrade channel, since the\n * attacker's own file then chooses how much work their guess costs.\n *\n * <p>Every field is range-checked <em>before</em> the KDF runs, and a rejected parameter never causes\n * the derivation to happen: reading {@code iterations = 2000000000} from a hostile file and then\n * obediently grinding through it is a denial of service delivered by configuration.\n *\n * <p><strong>Not a rate limiter.</strong> The KDF makes each guess expensive; it does not cap the\n * number of guesses. If an attacker can submit passphrases through your application, throttle in the\n * caller, per source and per principal, and count failures - this component has no idea who is asking.\n *\n * <p>Passphrases are {@code char[]}, copied on construction, erased by {@link #destroy()}. No\n * {@code String} overload exists, because a {@code String} passphrase cannot be erased.\n */\npublic final class PasswordKeySource implements KeySource, AutoCloseable {\n\n    /** Salt length for the KDF. Random per container, so identical passphrases differ per file. */\n    public static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n\n    /** Default PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 iterations; roughly 0.8-1.0 s on this JDK. */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 600_000;\n\n    private static final byte[] MAGIC = {'S', 'P', 'K', 'B', '1'};\n    private static final byte KDF_PBKDF2_SHA256 = 1;\n    private static final String KDF_NAME = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    private static final int HEADER_BYTES = 5 + 1 + 4 + 1 + SALT_BYTES + 12 + 4;\n    private static final int WRAPPED_BYTES = KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES + (Limits.TAG_BITS / 8);\n    private static final int FILE_BYTES = HEADER_BYTES + WRAPPED_BYTES;\n\n    private final Path file;\n    private final int keyVersion;\n    private final char[] passphrase;\n    private volatile boolean destroyed;\n\n    /**\n     * @param file an existing container written by {@link #create}\n     * @param keyVersion version to tag loaded keys with, {@code 1..255}; The file's privacy is\n     * re-checked before every read.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException with {@code AUTHENTICATION_FAILED} for a wrong passphrase, or\n     *     {@code INVALID_KEY} / It deliberately leaves the caller's array alone,\n     *       so erasing that is the holder's job, which {@link #destroy()} does;</li>\n     *   <li>the key object returned by {@code generateSecret} cannot be erased: {@code destroy()} on it\n     *       throws {@code DestroyFailedException}, leaves {@code isDestroyed()} false and zeroes neither\n     *       its own copy of the passphrase nor its derived-key field. It is therefore not called -\n     *       calling it would read as a control that does nothing. *\n * <p>Layout: a {@code SecretKeyEntry} holding a <em>random</em> AES-256 key, protected by the key\n * password, inside a store whose integrity MAC is computed from the store password. Nothing is derived\n * from the passphrase, so the key's entropy does not depend on passphrase wording, and no verification\n * pattern is needed: the MAC fails on a wrong store password and {@code getKey} fails on a wrong key\n * password.\n *\n * <p><strong>What this does not buy you.</strong> PKCS#12 protects both with a password-based KDF\n * whose iteration count this build does not control, and measurement on OpenJDK&nbsp;17.0.20.1 puts it\n * at roughly {@code 10^4} iterations - about 20&nbsp;ms per guess, five orders of magnitude cheaper\n * than a 600&nbsp;000-iteration PBKDF2. That gap is the entire offline-brute-force defence, so this\n * class is only safe when the \"passphrase\" is a high-entropy machine secret. It refuses anything\n * shorter than {@value #MIN_PASSPHRASE_CHARS} characters, and\n * {@link RandomPasswords#generate()} exists so nobody has to invent one by hand.\n *\n * <p>For a human-memorable passphrase, do not use this class. Generate a random key with\n * {@link SymmetricCrypto#generateKey(int)} and keep it in a KMS or HSM, or protect it with a\n * deliberately expensive KDF that you configure yourself.\n *\n * <p><strong>It is also a container, not a lock.</strong> A file-based key store protects a key only\n * from a reader who lacks the password. It does not hide the key from this process, so it adds nothing\n * against an attacker who can already read process memory, and it does not stop whoever supplies both\n * the file and the password from reading every record ever written.\n *\n * <p>File handling: the store must satisfy {@link PrivateFiles} - owner-only regular file, no symlink,\n * private ancestry - and that is re-checked on every {@link #load()}, not just at construction.\n * {@link #create} writes it private from the first byte, because {@code KeyStore.store()} would\n * otherwise leave the new file at {@code 0644} until a later chmod.\n *\n * <p>Passwords are {@code char[]}, copied on construction and erased by {@link #destroy()}. ALIAS_PATTERN);\n        }\n        if (keyVersion < EncryptedBlob.MIN_KEY_VERSION || keyVersion > EncryptedBlob.MAX_KEY_VERSION) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT, \"key version is out of range\");\n        }\n        char[] sp = requirePassphrase(storePassword, \"store password\");\n        char[] kp = null;\n        byte[] raw = new byte[KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES];\n        try {\n            kp = requirePassphrase(keyPassword, \"key password\");\n            Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n            if (parent == null || !Files.exists(parent)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key store directory does not exist; create it privately first\");\n            }\n            PrivateFiles.requireSecureDirectory(parent);\n            if (Files.exists(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key store already exists; refusing to overwrite it\");\n            }\n\n            new SecureRandom().nextBytes(raw);\n            KeyStore ks = KeyStore.getInstance(STORE_TYPE);\n            ks.load(null, null);\n            ks.setKeyEntry(alias, new SecretKeySpec(raw, \"AES\"), kp, null);\n\n            /* Created 0600 rather than chmod-ed afterwards: it must never exist world-readable. */\n            try (OutputStream           throw e;\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.PROVIDER_FAILURE,\n                    \"key store could not Arrays.fill(kp, (char) 0);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Loads the key. The store is re-read from disk each time - no key material is cached - and its\n     * file permissions are re-checked first.\n     *\n     * <p>Every failure is {@code KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE}, {@code INVALID_KEY} or {@code KEY_UNAVAILABLE}\n     * behind the same generic user message, so a wrong store password, a wrong key password, a\n     * tampered file and a missing alias cannot be told apart through {@code getMessage()}.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public KeyMaterial load() throws CryptoException {\n        if (destroyed) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_NOT_ACTIVE, \"key source is closed\");\n        }\n        PrivateFiles.requirePrivateKeyFile(file);\n        KeyStore ks;\n        try {\n            ks = KeyStore.getInstance(STORE_TYPE);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.PROVIDER_FAILURE,\n                    \"the PKCS12 keystore type is unavailable on this JVM\", e);\n        }\n        try {\n            if (Files.size(file) > MAX_STORE_BYTES) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE, \"key store is too large\");\n            }\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"key store could not be inspected\", e);\n        }\n        try (InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(file)) {\n            /*\n             * Both types together: on PKCS#12 a wrong store password surfaces as an IOException\n             * wrapping an integrity failure, not as a GeneralSecurityException, and a handler that\n             * catches only the latter would let it escape unclassified.\n             */\n            ks.load(in, storePassword);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"key store could not be opened: wrong store password, tampered file, or unreadable (\"\n                            + e.getClass().getSimpleName() + \")\", e);\n        }\n        try {\n            if (ks.size() > MAX_ENTRIES) {\n (!ks.containsAlias(alias)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_UNAVAILABLE,\n                        \"key store has no entry named \" + alias);\n            }\n            if (!ks.entryInstanceOf(alias, KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry.class)) {\n                /* Guards against a secret stored under a PrivateKeyEntry, which getKey would reject. */\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                        \"key store entry \" + alias + \" is not a symmetric key entry\");\n            }\n           \"key store entry \" + alias + \" is not a symmetric key\");\n            }\n            byte[] raw = sk.getEncoded();\n            if (raw == null) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                        \"key store entry \" + alias + \" cannot be exported\");\n            }\n            try {\n  } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            /* UnrecoverableKeyException: wrong key password. */\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"key could not be recovered: wrong key password or unreadable entry (\"\n                            + Further {@link #load()} calls fail closed. */\n    /** Same as {@link #destroy()}; lets callers use try-with-resources, which is the easy path. */\n    @Override\n    public void close() {\n        destroy();\n    }\n\n    public synchronized void destroy() {\n        Arrays.fill(storePassword, (char) 0);\n        Arrays.fill(keyPassword, (char) 0);\n        destroyed = true;\n    }\n\n    public boolean isDestroyed() {\n        return destroyed;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates a password and returns a private copy.\n     *\n     * <p>Two JDK behaviours drive the checks, both verified on OpenJDK&nbsp;17.0.20.1: a trailing\n     * {@code NUL} is dropped and every unpaired surrogate is mapped to {@code '?'} when the JCE\n     * converts a {@code char[]} to the KDF input, so passphrases in those classes derive the\n     * <em>same</em> key material as different ones; and the store's MAC is the only thing standing\n     * between an attacker and the key, so two passphrases that collapse to one are one lock with two\n     * keys. Both are refused rather than trimmed - trimming would silently change the secret and make\n     * an existing store unreadable.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if null, empty, shorter than {@value #MIN_PASSPHRASE_CHARS}, longer than\n     *     {@value #MAX_PASSPHRASE_CHARS}, ending in {@code NUL}, or containing an unpaired surrogate\n     */\n    static char[] requirePassphrase(char[] password, String what) throws CryptoException {\n        if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.MISSING_INPUT, what + \" is missing\");\n        }\n        if (password.length < MIN_PASSPHRASE_CHARS || password.length > MAX_PASSPHRASE_CHARS) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    what + \" must be between \" + MIN_PASSPHRASE_CHARS + \" and \" + MAX_PASSPHRASE_CHARS\n                            + \" characters. Generate it randomly; do not compose it by hand.\");\n        }\n        if (password[password.length - 1] == 0) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                    what + \" ends with a NUL character, which the JCE silently discards\");\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < password.length; i++) {\n            char c = password[i];\n            if (Character.isHighSurrogate(c)) {\n                if (i + 1 >= password.length || !Character.isLowSurrogate(password[i + 1])) {\n                    throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                            what + \" contains an unpaired surrogate, which the JCE maps to '?'\");\n                }\n                i++;\n            } else if (Character.isLowSurrogate(c)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_INPUT,\n                        what + \" contains an unpaired surrogate, which the JCE maps to '?'\");\n            }\n        }\n        return password.clone();\n    }\n\n    /** The store path and alias. A path is not a secret; the contents are. */\n    @Override\n    public String describe() {\n        return \"pkcs12:\" + file.toAbsolutePath() + \"#\" + alias + \"@\" + keyVersion;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Identity for {@link KeyVault}'s cache key. Deliberately excludes the passwords: a source's\n     * identity is where it reads from, and deriving a hash over secret material to compare sources\n     * would be a oracle-shaped footgun for no benefit.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(Object o) {\n        return o instanceof KeystoreKeySource other\n                && keyVersion == other.keyVersion\n                && file.equals(other.file)\n                && alias.equals(other.alias);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int hashCode() {\n        return Objects.hash(file, alias, keyVersion);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return describe();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"e0b3320f-39e0-4f1b-bffd-ee03ba48e9bc","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_3701edffa04840bba685d8dc","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.crypto;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.nio.channels.FileChannel;\nimport java.nio.file.FileSystemException;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFileAttributes;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\nimport java.util.EnumSet;\nimport java.util.Set;\n\n/**\n * Filesystem checks for anything holding key material or a password.\n *\n * <p>Rule 5 of the brief says secrets go \"only to files created private to the owner\". Checking the\n * mode after writing does not achieve that - there is a window between creation and the chmod - so\n * every file this component writes is created through {@link FileChannel#open} with its permissions\n * in the creation attributes, which means it never exists in a readable state.\n *\n * <p>Reading is the harder direction, because the file an attacker can influence is the one already\n * on disk. A mode check alone is bypassable: replace the file with a symlink to something else, put\n * it on a filesystem mounted read-only with stale attributes, or leave it in a world-writable\n * directory where anyone can swap it. So a key file must satisfy all of:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>it is a regular file and not a symlink - checked on the same inode the read will use, via\n *       {@code NOFOLLOW_LINKS};</li>\n *   <li>its mode grants nothing to group or other, and no execute or set-id bit is set;</li>\n *   <li>its owner is the user running this process;</li>\n *   <li>every ancestor directory is owned by that user, not writable by group or other, and either\n *       not world-writable or carrying the sticky bit (the {@code /tmp} convention);</li>\n *   <li>if the parent directory is writable by others, the read is re-validated afterwards: the\n *       directory's mode must be unchanged, the file's {@code (st_dev, st_ino)} pair must identify the\n *       same inode that passed the checks above, and its mode and owner must be unchanged - so unlinking\n *       and re-creating the entry while the read is in progress is detected and the bytes discarded.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>On a filesystem with no POSIX permissions (Windows without ACL support, FAT, some mounts) the\n * checks cannot be performed and this component refuses rather than assuming the file is private.\n * Configure a hardware-backed store or a secret manager there instead; see {@link KeystoreKeySource}.\n *\n * <p>Residual risk stated plainly: root can read any file this or any other process can read, and an\n * attacker who can already read the process's memory does not need the file. These guards raise the\n * cost against unprivileged local users, service accounts and configuration mistakes - they are not\n * a defence against a compromised host.\n */\npublic final class PrivateFiles {\n\n    private static final int S_ISUID = 04000;\n    private static final int S_ISGID = 02000;\n    private static final int S_ISVTX = 01000;\n    private static final int PERMS_MASK = 07777;\n\n    /** Owner read/write only: {@code 0600}. */\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n            EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n\n    /** Owner read/write/execute: {@code 0700}. Used when this component creates a directory. */\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY_DIR = EnumSet.of(\n            PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_EXECUTE);\n\n    private PrivateFiles() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Creates {@code file} with mode {@code 0600} in one step and writes {@code content}, refusing if\n     * it already exists. The parent directory must already satisfy {@link #requireSecureDirectory};\n     * that check is deliberately not relaxed for a directory the caller owns but did not create.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the file exists, the parent directory is not private, or the\n     *     filesystem does not support POSIX permissions\n     */\n    public static void writeOwnerOnlyFile(Path file, byte[] content) throws CryptoException {\n        Limits.requirePayload(content, Limits.MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES, \"file content\");\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        if (parent == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file has no parent directory\");\n        }\n        if (!Files.exists(parent, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file directory does not exist: refusing to create it implicitly\");\n        }\n        requireSecureDirectory(parent);\n        /*\n         * No \"does it already exist?\" check here on purpose. Asking and then opening are two decisions\n         * about one fact, and a local attacker can create the file in between, so the answer to the first\n         * question would not be the answer that governed the write. CREATE_NEW makes the filesystem\n         * decide, in the one operation that also creates the file with its 0600 mode, so there is no\n         * window to lose. A caller that wants replacement must delete first, deliberately.\n         */\n        try {\n            Set<StandardOpenOption> opts = EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW, StandardOpenOption.WRITE);\n            try (FileChannel ch = FileChannel.open(file, opts,\n                    java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY))) {\n                java.nio.ByteBuffer buf = java.nio.ByteBuffer.wrap(content);\n                while (buf.hasRemaining()) {\n                    ch.write(buf);\n                }\n                ch.force(true);\n            }\n        } catch (java.nio.file.FileAlreadyExistsException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file already exists; refusing to overwrite it\", e);\n        } catch (java.nio.file.AccessDeniedException | SecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created: access denied\", e);\n        } catch (FileSystemException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created\", e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created\", e);\n        }\n        /* Belt and braces: confirm the created file really is private before returning. */\n        requirePrivateKeyFile(file);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads at most {@code maxBytes} from a file that has been proven private to this user.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if any check in {@link #requirePrivateKeyFile} fails, or the file is\n     *     larger than {@code maxBytes}\n     */\n    public static byte[] readOwnerOnlyFile(Path file, int maxBytes) throws CryptoException {\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        if (parent == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file has no parent directory\");\n        }\n        boolean parentShared = isWritableByOthers(parent);\n        int parentModeBefore = unixMode(parent);\n        requirePrivateKeyFile(file);\n        /*\n         * Snapshot what the validation above was actually about - this inode, this mode - so the\n         * after-the-fact check can say something true. Comparing the parent directory's mode alone would\n         * not notice the attack it claims to notice: an attacker who may write the directory does not\n         * chmod it to swap a key out, he size = Files.size(file);\n            if (size > maxBytes) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE,\n                        \"key file is larger than \" + maxBytes + \" bytes\");\n            }\n            out = Files.readAllBytes(file);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"key file could not be read\", e);\n        }\n        if (out.length > maxBytes) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE,\n                    \"key file is larger than \" + maxBytes + \" bytes\");\n        }\n        if (parentShared) {\n            if (unixMode(parent) != parentModeBefore) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file directory changed during the read\");\n            }\n            long[] inodeAfter = identity(file);\n            if (inodeAfter[0] != inodeBefore[0] || inodeAfter[1] != inodeBefore[1]) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file was replaced during the read\");\n            }\n            if (unixMode(file) != modeBefore || !stillOwnedByMe(file)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file permissions or ownership
