Checking the\n * mode after writing does not achieve that - there is a window between creation and the chmod - so\n * every file this component writes is created through {@link FileChannel#open} with its permissions\n * in the creation attributes, which means it never exists in a readable state.\n *\n * <p>Reading is the harder direction, because the file an attacker can influence is the one already\n * on disk. A mode check alone is bypassable: replace the file with a symlink to something else, put\n * it on a filesystem mounted read-only with stale attributes, or leave it in a world-writable\n * directory where anyone can swap it. So a key file must satisfy all of:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>it is a regular file and not a symlink - checked on the same inode the read will use, via\n *       {@code NOFOLLOW_LINKS};</li>\n *   <li>its mode grants nothing to group or other, and no execute or set-id bit is set;</li>\n *   <li>its owner is the user running this process;</li>\n *   <li>every ancestor directory is owned by that user, not writable by group or other, and either\n *       not world-writable or carrying the sticky bit (the {@code /tmp} convention);</li>\n *   <li>if the parent directory is writable by others, the read is re-validated afterwards: the\n *       directory's mode must be unchanged, the file's {@code (st_dev, st_ino)} pair must identify the\n *       same inode that passed the checks above, and its mode and owner must be unchanged - so unlinking\n *       and re-creating the entry while the read is in progress is detected and the bytes discarded.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>On a filesystem with no POSIX permissions (Windows without ACL support, FAT, some mounts) the\n * checks cannot be performed and this component refuses rather than assuming the file is private.\n * Configure a hardware-backed store or a secret manager there instead; see {@link KeystoreKeySource}.\n *\n * <p>Residual risk stated plainly: root can read any file this or any other process can read, and an\n * attacker who can already read the process's memory does not need the file. These guards raise the\n * cost against unprivileged local users, service accounts and configuration mistakes - they are not\n * a defence against a compromised host.\n */\npublic final class PrivateFiles {\n\n    private static final int S_ISUID = 04000;\n    private static final int S_ISGID = 02000;\n    private static final int S_ISVTX = 01000;\n    private static final int PERMS_MASK = 07777;\n\n    /** Owner read/write only: {@code 0600}. */\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n            EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n\n    /** Owner read/write/execute: {@code 0700}. Used when this component creates a directory. */\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY_DIR = EnumSet.of(\n            PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_EXECUTE);\n\n    private PrivateFiles() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Creates {@code file} with mode {@code 0600} in one step and writes {@code content}, refusing if\n     * it already exists. The parent directory must already satisfy {@link #requireSecureDirectory};\n     * that check is deliberately not relaxed for a directory the caller owns but did not create.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the file exists, the parent directory is not private, or the\n     *     filesystem does not support POSIX permissions\n     */\n    public static void writeOwnerOnlyFile(Path file, byte[] content) throws CryptoException {\n        Limits.requirePayload(content, Limits.MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES, \"file content\");\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        if (parent == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file has no parent directory\");\n        }\n        if (!Files.exists(parent, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file directory does not exist: refusing to create it implicitly\");\n        }\n        requireSecureDirectory(parent);\n        /*\n         * No \"does it already exist?\" check here on purpose. Asking and then opening are two decisions\n         * about one fact, and a local attacker can create the file in between, so the answer to the first\n         * question would not be the answer that governed the write. CREATE_NEW makes the filesystem\n         * decide, in the one operation that also creates the file with its 0600 mode, so there is no\n         * window to lose. A caller that wants replacement must delete first, deliberately.\n         */\n        try {\n            Set<StandardOpenOption> opts = EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW, StandardOpenOption.WRITE);\n            try (FileChannel ch = FileChannel.open(file, opts,\n                    java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY))) {\n                java.nio.ByteBuffer buf = java.nio.ByteBuffer.wrap(content);\n                while (buf.hasRemaining()) {\n                    ch.write(buf);\n                }\n                ch.force(true);\n            }\n        } catch (java.nio.file.FileAlreadyExistsException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file already exists; refusing to overwrite it\", e);\n        } catch (java.nio.file.AccessDeniedException | SecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created: access denied\", e);\n        } catch (FileSystemException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created\", e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file could not be created\", e);\n        }\n        /* Belt and braces: confirm the created file really is private before returning. */\n        requirePrivateKeyFile(file);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads at most {@code maxBytes} from a file that has been proven private to this user.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if any check in {@link #requirePrivateKeyFile} fails, or the file is\n     *     larger than {@code maxBytes}\n     */\n    public static byte[] readOwnerOnlyFile(Path file, int maxBytes) throws CryptoException {\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        if (parent == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file has no parent directory\");\n        }\n        boolean parentShared = isWritableByOthers(parent);\n        int parentModeBefore = unixMode(parent);\n        requirePrivateKeyFile(file);\n        /*\n         * Snapshot what the validation above was actually about - this inode, this mode - so the\n         * after-the-fact check can say something true. Comparing the parent directory's mode alone would\n         * not notice the attack it claims to notice: an attacker who may write the directory does not\n         * chmod it to swap a key out, he unlinks the entry and creates another one.\n         */\n        long[] inodeBefore = identity(file);\n        int modeBefore = unixMode(file);\n        byte[] out;\n        try {\n            long size = Files.size(file);\n            if (size > maxBytes) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE,\n                        \"key file is larger than \" + maxBytes + \" bytes\");\n            }\n            out = Files.readAllBytes(file);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"key file could not be read\", e);\n        }\n        if (out.length > maxBytes) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.TOO_LARGE,\n                    \"key file is larger than \" + maxBytes + \" bytes\");\n        }\n        if (parentShared) {\n            if (unixMode(parent) != parentModeBefore) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file directory changed during the read\");\n            }\n            long[] inodeAfter = identity(file);\n            if (inodeAfter[0] != inodeBefore[0] || inodeAfter[1] != inodeBefore[1]) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file was replaced during the read\");\n            }\n            if (unixMode(file) != modeBefore || !stillOwnedByMe(file)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file permissions or ownership changed during the read\");\n            }\n        }\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code {st_dev, st_ino}} for {@code p} - enough to tell \"the same file\" from \"a different file\n     * that happens to have the same name\", which is the distinction that matters here.\n     */\n    private static long[] identity(Path p) throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            Object dev = Files.getAttribute(p, \"unix:dev\", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n            Object ino = Files.getAttribute(p, \"unix:ino\", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n            if (!(dev instanceof Long d) || !(ino instanceof Long i)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"filesystem does not report inode identity for \" + p);\n            }\n            return new long[] {d, i};\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"inode identity could not be read for \" + p, e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"inode identity could not be read for \" + p, e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Ownership re-check that reports rather than throws, for use after the read. */\n    private static boolean stillOwnedByMe(Path p) {\n        String me = System.getProperty(\"user.name\");\n        try {\n            return me != null && me.equals(Files.getOwner(p, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS).getName());\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies {@code file} is a private, non-symlink, owner-only regular file.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException naming the failed requirement in {@code getTechnicalDetail()}\n     */\n    public static void requirePrivateKeyFile(Path file) throws CryptoException {\n        PosixFileAttributes attrs = readPosix(file);\n        if (attrs.isSymbolicLink()) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file is a symbolic link\");\n        }\n        if (!attrs.isRegularFile()) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file is not a regular file\");\n        }\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> perms = attrs.permissions();\n        for (PosixFilePermission p : perms) {\n            switch (p) {\n                case OWNER_READ:\n                case OWNER_WRITE:\n                    break;\n                default:\n                    /* Group/other access, or any execute bit on a data file: refuse. */\n                    throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                            \"key file permissions grant more than owner read/write: \"\n                                    + java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions.toString(perms));\n            }\n        }\n        int mode = unixMode(file);\n        if ((mode & (S_ISUID | S_ISGID)) != 0) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"key file has a set-id bit set\");\n        }\n        requireOwns(file, \"key file\");\n        requireSecureDirectory(file.toAbsolutePath().getParent());\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies a directory cannot be written to by anyone but its owner, allowing the sticky-bit\n     * convention for shared temporary directories.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the directory is writable by group or other without a sticky bit, is not\n     *     owned by this user, is not a directory, or is a symlink\n     */\n    public static void requireSecureDirectory(Path dir) throws CryptoException {\n        if (dir == null) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"directory is missing\");\n        }\n        Path abs = dir.toAbsolutePath();\n        /* Walk from the shortest prefix down: a private file inside an open directory is not private. */\n        for (Path component = abs; component != null; ) {\n            PosixFileAttributes attrs = readPosix(component);\n            if (attrs.isSymbolicLink()) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file directory path traverses a symbolic link: \" + component);\n            }\n            boolean isRoot = component.getParent() == null;\n            if (!isRoot && !attrs.isDirectory()) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file directory path component is not a directory: \" + component);\n            }\n            int mode = unixMode(component) & PERMS_MASK;\n            boolean sticky = (mode & S_ISVTX) != 0;\n            /*\n             * Group and other write access are treated as one condition, because they are the same\n             * threat: somebody else can unlink or replace an entry. The sticky bit is what removes that\n             * power - only the owner of an entry may delete or rename it - which is why /tmp is 1777 and\n             * why the check is \"writable by others without a sticky bit\" rather than \"world-writable\".\n             *\n             * Rejecting group-writable outright would be stricter, not safer: /tmp is group-writable on\n             * every conventional system, so a blanket refusal rejects the standard temporary directory\n             * while a 0775 directory with no sticky bit - genuinely unsafe - is refused for a different\n             * reason than the one that matters. One rule, applied to both, is the one that can be\n             * reasoned about.\n             */\n            boolean writableByOthers = (mode & 0022) != 0;\n            if (writableByOthers && !sticky) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"key file directory is writable by others without the sticky bit: \" + component);\n            }\n            if (!isRoot) {\n                requireOwns(component, \"key file directory\");\n            }\n            component = component.getParent();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Opens a new owner-only ({@code 0600}) output stream, creating the file in the same step that\n     * sets its permissions. For callers such as {@code KeyStore.store()} that must write a secret\n     * themselves and cannot pass creation attributes: {@code Files.newOutputStream} has no\n     * {@code FileAttribute} overload, so a store written that way is born {@code 0644} and stays\n     * readable until a later chmod - which is a window, not a fix.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the file exists or the parent directory is not private\n     */\n    public static java.io.OutputStream newOwnerOnlyOutputStream(Path file) throws CryptoException {\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        if (parent == null || !Files.exists(parent, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"target directory does not exist\");\n        }\n        requireSecureDirectory(parent);\n        /* As in writeOwnerOnlyFile: CREATE_NEW is the decision, so nothing can be swapped in between. */\n        try {\n            Set<StandardOpenOption> opts = EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW, StandardOpenOption.WRITE);\n            return java.nio.channels.Channels.newOutputStream(\n                    FileChannel.open(file, opts,\n                            java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY)));\n        } catch (java.nio.file.FileAlreadyExistsException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"file already exists; refusing to overwrite it\", e);\n        } catch (IOException | SecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"private file could not be opened for writing\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Creates a directory that only the owner can enter, failing if it already exists. */\n    public static Path createOwnerOnlyDirectory(Path dir) throws CryptoException {\n        Path parent = dir.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        requireSecureDirectory(parent);\n        try {\n            Path created = Files.createDirectory(dir,\n                    java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY_DIR));\n            requireSecureDirectory(created);\n            return created;\n        } catch (IOException | SecurityException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"private directory could not be created\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** True if users other than the owner may create or unlink entries in {@code dir}. */\n    static boolean isWritableByOthers(Path dir) throws CryptoException {\n        int mode = unixMode(dir) & PERMS_MASK;\n        return (mode & 0022) != 0;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The file's {@code st_mode}. Read directly rather than derived from\n     * {@link PosixFilePermission} because that enum cannot represent the set-id or sticky bits -\n     * {@code PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rwxrwxrwt\")} throws - and those are exactly the bits\n     * that matter for a swap attack.\n     *\n     * @throws CryptoException if the filesystem exposes no unix attributes: no POSIX permissions means\n     *     privacy cannot be verified, so this component refuses instead of assuming\n     */\n    static int unixMode(Path p) throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            Object v = Files.getAttribute(p, \"unix:mode\", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n            if (!(v instanceof Integer i)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                        \"filesystem does not report unix permissions for \" + p);\n            }\n            return i;\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"filesystem does not support POSIX permissions, so key-file privacy cannot be \"\n                            + \"verified: \" + p, e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"permissions could not be read for \" + p, e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static PosixFileAttributes readPosix(Path p) throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            return Files.readAttributes(p, PosixFileAttributes.class, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"filesystem does not support POSIX permissions, so key-file privacy cannot be \"\n                            + \"verified: \" + p, e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"file status could not be read: \" + p, e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void requireOwns(Path p, String what) throws CryptoException {\n        String me = System.getProperty(\"user.name\");\n        String owner;\n        try {\n            owner = Files.getOwner(p, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS).getName();\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    \"ownership cannot be verified on this filesystem for \" + p, e);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE,\n                    \"ownership could not be read for \" + p, e);\n        }\n        if (me == null || !me.equals(owner)) {\n            /*\n             * The owner check is best-effort by design: it stops the common attack (a key file planted\n             * by another service account in a shared directory) but an attacker who can set file\n             * ownership is already root. Note that this means a process cannot run as one user and read\n             * a key file created by another, however legitimately - that is the intended trade.\n             */\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INSECURE_KEY_STORAGE,\n                    what + \" is owned by \" + owner + \", not by the running user \" + me);\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"8ae64c44-ec21-44a8-a687-b7b4c4816dc3","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"8ae64c44-ec21-44a8-a687-b7b4c4816dc3","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now KeystoreKeySource full read with line numbers. KeyVault facts:\n- `sourceFor` (line ~353) takes `synchronized (rotationLock)` at 358, and the cached-hit return is INSIDE the lock (line ~364-366: `KeySource existing = sources.get(keyVersion); if (existing != null) return existing;`). So EVERY decrypt acquires the global rotationLock — including hot-path decrypts of cached versions.\n- `factory.create(keyVersion)` (line 388) runs while holding rotationLock. `source.load()` happens in decryptWith outside the lock.\n\nSo: (1) every decrypt serializes on one global lock (throughput, and any slow factory.create blocks all versions); (2) factory.create is a caller-supplied callback invoked under the lock — README rule 7 claims concurrency safety, but a factory that does any I/O (e.g., fetching from a secret manager, which is a natural factory!) stalls every concurrent decrypt and encrypt. The PBKDF2 derive happens in load() (outside lock) — but a factory that eagerly loads (e.g. `v -> PasswordKeySource.loadOnce(...)`) or a factory that constructs a KeystoreKeySource requires... KeystoreKeySource constructor just stores paths/passwords. EnvKeySource constructor reads env? Possibly getenv at load(). KeyFileKeySource just stores path. So default factories are cheap. But the factory interface contract invites a source that validates/loads at create time; and `KeyVault.of(...)` exists. Also `factory.create()` could `new PasswordKeySource(path, pass, ver)` which copies char[] — cheap.\n\nSeverity: Low-Medium (CWE-400 uncontrolled resource consumption / lock contention DoS via attacker-triggered cold misses). Combined with: an attacker can force a cold miss for any *provisioned* version by... no — cache misses only happen once per version (cached thereafter). Except: `release(source)` in finally when `!isCached(source, keyVersion)` — for decoys only. Cache persists until rotation/disable. So an attacker can trigger at most one factory call per provisioned version (amortized). Hmm, that weakens the DoS: the expensive part (load/derive) happens per-decrypt outside the lock anyway, which the README documents (rule 13: \"a cheap call can become an expensive one... attacker who can make you miss can multiply your CPU\"). Wait — is load() cached? No: KeyVault loads the key per operation by design (cache holds sources, not keys). So every decrypt of a provisioned version runs source.load() → PBKDF2 derive (~0.5-3s) each time! That's the documented CPU amplification (README rule 13 says so explicitly, and assigns rate limiting to the caller). So the real \"code\" issue: rotationLock held during factory.create is minor; the per-op KDF is documented-by-design with caller obligation.\n\nI'll fold these into a single finding about lock scope + per-operation cost? The README explicitly documents the per-op cost as accepted design with caller obligation (rate limit in front). As a reviewer I could report the rotationLock-across-factory.create as a Low finding (CWE-400/CWE-667): global lock held across an unbounded caller-supplied callback; a factory that performs I/O (a legitimate implementation — fetching a key from a secret manager is the natural use of `KeySourceFactory`) serializes the entire vault's encrypt+decrypt path, turning one slow remote call into a fleet-wide stall. Fix: build outside the lock and publish with putIfAbsent (the loser releases), keeping only the map mutation inside.\n\nWait — careful: creating outside the lock then putIfAbsent is exactly what the code does *inside*; moving creation outside the lock is safe (putIfAbsent resolves races; loser released by caller's finally via isCached). com.example.crypto;\n\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.util.Base64;\n\n/**\n * Parses key material out of a byte buffer that came from configuration or a file.\n *\n * <p>Two shapes are accepted, and only these two:\n * <ol>\n *   <li>exactly {@value KeyMaterial#KEY_BYTES} raw bytes;</li>\n *   <li>canonical base64url text without padding, decoding to exactly\n *       {@value KeyMaterial#KEY_BYTES} bytes, with no whitespace anywhere.</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <p>Why the encoding rules are strict:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@code Base64.getMimeDecoder()} silently discards every character outside the alphabet, so\n *       {@code \"not-a-key!\"} decodes to five bytes with no error and would become a truncated,\n *       weak key. The strict decoder is used and the result is re-encoded and compared, so a\n *       non-canonical spelling is refused rather than silently accepted;</li>\n *   <li>no whitespace is accepted <em>anywhere</em>, in either form. A trailing newline is refused in\n *       the text form as well as the raw one: byte {@code 0x0A} is not printable, so it is rejected\n *       before the decoder is consulted. Trimming first is not an option - a value whose leading or\n *       trailing characters are removable is a value whose length somebody else decides, and the length\n *       is what distinguishes the raw form from the text form. The two forms are distinguishable by\n *       length alone, so there is no ambiguity to resolve and no \"try both and hope\" path;</li>\n *   <li>a buffer that is neither is rejected outright. There is no fallback that pads, truncates, or\n *       hashes the input down to 32 bytes - silently deriving a key from a malformed one is how a key\n *       rotation ends up with two different keys called the same thing.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>There is deliberately no counterpart that renders key material as a {@code String}: a\n * {@code String} cannot be zeroed, so it would leave the key in the heap past the end of the\n * operation. {@link KeyMaterial#copyForWrite()} is the only export path and it hands back a byte array\n * the caller is expected to wipe.\n */\npublic final class KeyEncoding {\n\n    /** Maximum size of a buffer this will look at: more than any accepted key form needs. */\n    public static final int MAX_INPUT_BYTES = 4096;\n\n    private static final Base64.Encoder CANON = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding();\n    private static final Base64.Decoder DECODER = Base64.getUrlDecoder();\n\n    private KeyEncoding() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @param raw bytes read from a key file or a configuration value; never null\n     * @return a new array of exactly {@value KeyMaterial#KEY_BYTES} bytes\n     * @throws CryptoException if the buffer is not one of the two accepted forms\n     */\n    public static byte[] decodeKeyBytes(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] in = Limits.requirePayload(raw, MAX_INPUT_BYTES, \"key material\");\n        if (in.length == KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES) {\n            return in.clone();\n        }\n        if (in.length == 0) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY, \"key material is empty\");\n        }\n        for (byte b : in) {\n            int v = b & 0xFF;\n            /* Control characters, including NUL and newline, are not part of the text form. */\n            if (v <= 0x20 || v > 0x7E) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                        \"key material is neither \" + KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES + \" raw bytes nor printable base64url text\");\n            }\n        }\n        String text = new String(in, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);\n        byte[] decoded;\n        try {\n            decoded = DECODER.decode(text);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                    \"key material is not valid base64url text\", e);\n        }\n        if (!CANON.encodeToString(decoded).equals(text)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                    \"key material is not canonically encoded base64url text\");\n        }\n        if (decoded.length != KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES) {\n            throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                    \"key material decodes to \" + decoded.length + \" bytes, expected \" + KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES);\n        }\n        return decoded;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Renders a key as canonical base64url text. For the one legitimate case: an operator-facing\n     * export used to place a key into a secret manager.\n     *\n     * <p>The result is a {@code String} and therefore <em>not</em> erasable; it exists so the caller\n     * can hand a key to a system that only accepts text. Callers must not log it, must not put it in a\n     * command line, and must not write it to a file they have not created with {@code 0600}\n     * permissions. Prefer {@code PrivateFiles.writeOwnerOnlyFile} with raw bytes.\n     *\n     * <p>{@link KeyMaterial} is required rather than a bare array so the caller has already had to\n     * hold the key in a managed object.\n     */\n    public static String toBase64Url(KeyMaterial key) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] raw = key.copyForWrite();\n        try {\n            return CANON.encodeToString(raw);\n        } finally {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"8186938f-2713-4de1-b3f8-b8989a6f111c","session_id":"d7e617cc-a2d8-4a3e-b993-59a9fa2fa4b9","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"8186938f-2713-4de1-b3f8-b8989a6f111c","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"KeyEncoding confirmed:\n- `decodeKeyBytes` at line 54: raw 32-byte path returns clone at ~56-58 with no entropy check.\n- Line 70: `String text = new String(in, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);` — key material in a String (non-erasable). Note UnixSystem is a com.sun internal (available on OpenJDK Linux, requires --add-exports? In practice `com.sun.security.auth.module.UnixSystem` is in jdk.security.auth module, exported. It's accessible with a module dependency; commonly used).\n\nI'll present a fix comparing unix:uid via UnixSystem, with fallback to refusing rather than trusting user.name:\n\n```java\n    private static long currentUid() throws CryptoException {\n        try {\n            return new com.sun.security.auth.module.UnixSystem().getUid();\n        } catch (LinkageError | UnsupportedOperationException e) {\n            throw new CryptoException(..., \"real user id is unavailable; refusing to guess ownership\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static boolean stillOwnedByMe(Path p) {\n        try {\n            Object uid = Files.getAttribute(p, \"unix:uid\", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n            return uid instanceof Long u && u == currentUid();\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n```\nCareful with `u == currentUid()` — Long unboxing to long is fine. Mention jdk.security.auth module requirement, and that a non-POSIX/non-UnixSystem fallback must fail closed.\n\nSEC-03 fix: entropy check on raw key bytes — reject low-entropy raw material (e.g., reject a raw 32-byte buffer that is not high-entropy; at minimum reject buffers that are all printable ASCII text — since a 32-char phrase would be printable — and require evidence of randomness). A concrete, defensible fix:\n\n```java\n    public static byte[] decodeKeyBytes(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] in = Limits.requirePayload(raw, MAX_INPUT_BYTES, \"key material\");\n        if (in.length == KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES) {\n            if (looksLikeText(in)) {\n                throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.INVALID_KEY,\n                        \"32 bytes of printable text is a passphrase, not a key: use PasswordKeySource\");\n            }\n            if (shannonEntropyBitsPerByte(in) < MIN_RAW_KEY_ENTROPY) {\n                throw new CryptoException(... \"raw key material is too predictable to be a 256-bit key\");\n            }\n            return in.clone();\n        }\n```\nwith helper methods sketched. Keep it reasonably concise but real.\n\nSEC-04 fix (nonce capacity unenforced): add a per-instance/per-key record counter with an enforced ceiling. Since KeyVault holds the per-key context, a pragmatic fix in SymmetricCrypto: track records encrypted per key version fingerprint with a hard stop, plus document. Better fix: enforce a counter keyed by key identity in SymmetricCrypto:\n\n```java\n    private static final long MAX_RECORDS_PER_KEY = 1L << 28; // ~268M, ~2^28: birthday margin ~2^-37 collision\n    private final ConcurrentHashMap<KeyFingerprint, AtomicLong> recordsPerKey = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n```\nHmm, that requires a key fingerprint (safe: first 8 bytes of HMAC of key? that's a key-derived value — but truncated keyed hash is fine). Simpler and honest: deterministic nonce construction (RFC 5114-style explicit-nonce / counter-based) or derive nonce = first 12 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(key, counter) with an enforced counter persisted per key version in KeyVault. Realistic minimal fix: enforce a counter in KeyVault.encrypt path with a persisted per-version counter and refuse beyond the ceiling:\n\n```java\n    private static final int MAX_RECORDS_PER_KEY = 1 << 26;\n\n    // KeyVault.encrypt(...)\n    long n = recordsIssued.incrementAndGet();\n    if (n > MAX_RECORDS_PER_KEY) {\n        throw new CryptoException(CryptoException.CryptoFailureCode.KEY_NOT_ACTIVE,\n                \"this key version has produced its record ceiling; rotate() before encrypting\");\n    }\n```\nwhere `recordsIssued` is a per-vault AtomicLong bound to the active version, reset by rotate(). Honest caveat: a stateless multi-process fleet needs the counter in shared storage; the fix at least makes the ceiling enforced within a process and turns the README's advice into a refusal. I'll note this.\n\nSEC-05 fix (Strings with key material): avoid materialising `String` in decodeKeyBytes — decode bytes directly with a byte-level base64url decoder, and for the canonical check re-encode into bytes and compare byte arrays. Fix should target the internal String creation in decodeKeyBytes: implement `decodeBase64UrlAscii(byte[])` operating on bytes + `encodeToBytes` comparison. Provide code:\n\n```java\n    public static byte[] decodeKeyBytes(byte[] raw) throws CryptoException {\n        byte[] in = Limits.requirePayload(raw, MAX_INPUT_BYTES, \"key material\");\n        if (in.length == KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES) {\n            return in.clone();\n        }\n        byte[] decoded = decodeBase64UrlAscii(in);      // byte-in, byte-out: no String is created\n        if (!java.util.Arrays.equals(CANON.encode(decoded), in)) {\n            throw new CryptoException(... \"key material is not canonically encoded base64url text\");\n        }\n```\nwhere `CANON.encode(decoded)` returns byte[] via `Base64.Encoder.encode(byte[])` — yes! `Base64.Encoder.encode(byte[])` has a byte[] overload, and `decode(byte[])` exists too. Code:\n\n```java\n    private KeySource sourceFor(int keyVersion) throws CryptoException {\n        ... range check, cache hit fast path outside the lock ...\n        KeySource existing = sources.get(keyVersion);\n        if (existing != null) return existing;\n        KeySource created = factory.create(keyVersion);   // no lock held\n        synchronized (rotationLock) {\n            requireOpen();\n            if (disabled.containsKey(keyVersion)) { release(created); throw ... }\n            if (keyVersion > highestIssued.get() || sources.size() >= MAX_VERSIONS) { release(created); return decoySource(...); }\n            KeySource winner = sources.putIfAbsent(keyVersion, created);\n            ...\n        }\n    }\n```\nNote the trade-off: an extra factory call may run concurrently for the same version (acceptable: putIfAbsent already handles the winner), and it must be released. Honest.\n\nSEC-07 fix (decoy cheaper than real load): make the decoy path cost-matched, e.g., always spend a fixed KDF-equivalent delay... Real fix options: (a) do the tag check against a constant-time dummy is inherently the same cost; the issue is the source-load cost difference. Fix: bound real-source load time into a fixed budget for the unprovisioned path — e.g., the decoy path performs a comparable fixed cost (a PBKDF2 with the same iteration count on a random input), i.e. equalize:\n\n```java\n    private static KeySource decoySource(int keyVersion) {\n        return new DecoySource(keyVersion);\n    }\n\n    private static final class DecoySource implements KeySource {\n        @Override\n        public KeyMaterial load() throws CryptoException {\n            byte[] filler = new byte[KeyMaterial.KEY_BYTES];\n            DECOY_RANDOM.nextBytes(filler);\n            // Cost-match a real PasswordKeySource cold load so the version estate is not\n            // measurable through response time.\n            byte[] pad = pbkdf2(filler, DECOY_SALT, PasswordKeySource.DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n            ...\n        }\n    }\n```\nThat costs CPU on every unprovisioned decrypt — which is actually what you want for a DoS-resistance trade-off... but it *increases* DoS amplification (attacker sends forged records naming provisioned-but-cold versions → forces full KDF each time). Hmm. Honest framing: this is a genuine trade-off; the alternative fix is to accept the oracle and remove the *distinct* observability by caching decoys... The README says it's a stated residual risk. My fix suggestion should be honest: either (a) pad the decoy to the real source's cost when the real source is a fast one, and additionally rate-limit decrypt per caller (README says rate limiting must be caller-side), or (b) pre-provision a source for every version in the issued range so no decoy path exists in that space, eliminating the cost difference for names an attacker can legitimately use. Option (b) is clean: the decoy path only fires for versions > highestIssued or beyond MAX_VERSIONS — where a \"not provisioned\" answer is not secret... wait, is it? If keyVersion > highestIssued → decoy. highestIssued is effectively public (encrypt uses active version, so records reveal issued versions). So an attacker learns only whether a version is between 1 and highestIssued but not cached... which is already observable via cold/warm timing. So the real leak is \"this issued version is not cached / not provisioned\" — of low value.\n\nGiven that, is SEC-07 worth reporting? The code itself flags it as residual risk. Also section 1 executive summary: overall assessment — this is a carefully written AEAD/key-handling library with no critical/high-impact remote-compromise defect; one High (local TOCTOU under a shared-parent directory), a Medium-High ownership-decision weakness, several Medium design gaps, and Low/informational items; no hardcoded keys, no weak algorithms.\n\nNote: for severity of SEC-01 — \"High\" is defensible: a local attacker with write access to the key-file directory can substitute the keystore between validation and load... wait, actually can they get key material? The swap attack means the *library* loads an attacker-supplied keystore, and then... the attacker's keystore has an attacker-known password, so the attacker learns which alias/label the app uses, and — more importantly — the attacker can cause the app to use an attacker-known key (key substitution → the attacker can then forge records that the app accepts, and any data encrypted under the substituted key is readable by the attacker). Also the reverse: it defeats the privacy checks the library claims. Also there's a subtler leak: the app decrypts with the attacker's key, so attacker-observable failure/success patterns... The main risk: an attacker with a shared-writable parent directory (e.g. /tmp, /run, a world-writable config dir) can silently substitute the domain key → full loss of confidentiality/integrity for records subsequently protected, plus the library's own threat model explicitly treats the shared-parent swap case as in-scope (hence the re-check in readOwnerOnlyFile). Also PasswordKeySource *does* use the guarded helper, so KeystoreKeySource is the outlier. High seems right, maybe Medium-High. I'll keep High but state the precondition clearly (attacker can write the keystore's parent directory and knows/guesses the path; store password is not required for the swap since the substituted file is the attacker's own).\n\nHmm — wait. To load a PKCS#12 store you need the store password; a swapped file encrypted under the attacker's password will fail to load with the app's real store password (integrity check fails). PKCS#12 MAC verification: ks.load(in, storePassword) verifies the MAC → mismatch → IOException → refused. Unless the attacker crafts a store with *no* MAC (PKCS#12 with MAC iteration count 0 / absent MAC is accepted by some parsers) — JDK's PKCS12 implementation: if the macData is absent, it doesn't verify. Yes — an attacker can create a PKCS#12 with no integrity MAC (e.g. openssl pkcs12 -nomac or -passout with empty + -nomac), and the JDK's PKCS12 keystore will load it without a password check (it tolerates absent MAC). Then the alias contents are attacker-chosen → key substitution succeeds. That's exactly the classic caveat. I should state that precisely but carefully: \"an integrity-MAC-less PKCS#12 file can be loaded without the store password being known (the JDK skips MAC verification when macData is absent), so the swap yields an attacker-chosen key for the alias\" — I'm fairly confident: PKCS12_key_store.load → if macData != null verify; with -nomac there is no macData and password isn't checked. Yes, that's a known behavior (and why \"no MAC\" stores are considered unauthenticated). I'll phrase it as \"unless the substituted file carries a MAC that matches the real store password — and a substituted file with no MAC at all is accepted without any password check by the JDK's PKCS#12 parser\" and note it should be verified. Good — honest hedging.\n\nAlternatively, even without a successful key substitution, the swap is a availability + integrity-of-intent failure and, importantly, the *PasswordKeySource* threat model considers this in-scope. I'll present both.\n\nAlso SEC-01 note: the tests include no keystore-swap-during-read test (unlike the wrong-password/0600 tests) — supporting evidence.\n\nOK.
